Dear lawmakers, it is possible to consider the law proposal we are discussing under three main topics. The first is to change the structure of the bank, and the second is to establish a development fund, and the third is to establish employee-related provisions. The Merkur law proposal aims to restructure Turkey's Development Bank Corporation to become a more effective partner in Turkey's growth and development. The proposal envisions changing the structure of the bank and organizing it like other public banks, establishing a new development fund and fund-related subsidiaries, and the funds can participate in companies, and it consists of provisions related to employment and transfer of employees. The law proposal aims to support investment and projects in line with our country's development goals, using modern development and investment bank tools, it says. Development and investment banking are intended to be actively active internationally, as in other countries' examples on this issue, in a situation that will contribute more to the development of the country and effectively use the funds available. Dear lawmakers, the issue of production is undoubtedly one of Turkey's most important problems. Increased investments and production, the development of our country is a matter that needs to be focused on the provision of our nation's well-being. As the National Movement Party, we care and support any effort that is being made in this framework. Because we believe that the good Turkish nation is worthy of the best, that it is one of the most important duties of the state to ensure that every citizen, regardless of which corner of the country, will have quality and rapid access to public services. We also consider the Law Proposal on Turkey's Development Bank Anonymous an attempt to contribute to the development of our country as a quest. The Development Bank, as it is known, was founded in 1975 as the State Industrial and Labour Investment Bank, and was named the Bank of Development of Turkey in 1988. At the same time, the opportunity to provide financing for other sectors along with the bank's industrial sector has been brought at that time. Since regulations with Law 4456 came into effect in 1999, it is clear that the existing position of the Bank of Development is not enough and that new regulations are being made in line with changes in the banking system and its objectives in the world and in our country. Development banks are local, national, regional or multinational financial institutions that provide long-term capital, mostly with technical and administrative support for producer sectors and infrastructure investments. In Article 3 of Banking Law 5411, the development and investment bank is defined as the branches of such institutions abroad and the branches of such institutions, which operate and perform the tasks given to them by specific laws, except to accept deposits or accession funds. Development financing institutions are institutions that operate on market principles by investing. In general, it is possible to sort development financing institutions according to their activities, according to the capital structures and the area they address. These are institutions that support directly the economic policies and plans of the state, as well as institutions that support some sectors or institutions or offer investment banking services. As it is known, in an economy where production is weak, the income of citizens is dwindling, the savings are not increased, and there is a shortage of financing. As the National Movement Party, it is our main goal to establish a stable economic growth and to establish a social welfare system that creates employment, creates employment by the establishment of a strong production economy, and ensures a fair share of every part of the value involved in the process, protecting the poor, making the revenue distribution fair. We support investment banking based on advanced technology, high export capacity, which will provide cheap and long-term funding for employment investments. Our election statement said that public banks would be able to provide services to modernise the agriculture sector, to boost industrial production, to address inter-regional development differences, to support agricultural production, small and medium-sized businesses, to support advanced technological investments, to ensure that the Turkish Development Bank is effectively used in restructuring and effectively in the financing of the COBI and to support projects aimed at resolving development between regions. In order to create a healthy investment, production, exports and employment chain, we see the development of development banking that provides long-term financing for investments. We find it important that investment projects will be supported by long-term financing, capital and technical assistance in order to create qualified capacity in production, boost competitiveness, access to scale economies and spread capital, and investments that will increase investments in infrastructure, environment, energy, research and development investments, sector efficiency and competition, financial applications for small and medium-sized enterprises and contribute to improving regional development differences. Dear lawmakers, the Turkish Development Bank has established its legal personality as well as the Turkish Development Fund, and its working procedures and fundamentals are decided by Turkey's Development Fund. It is also part of the proposal that the Development Fund can establish subsidized funds. The development fund established within this structure and the control of subsidiaries and their participation is also important. In the first instance of the proposal, the provision to exclude more than half of its capital from the provisions of companies owned by the public in the form of funds has been changed on the commission, as we called for it to violate Article 165 of the Constitution, and these are covered under the Court's supervision. The bank, on the other hand, can only be assigned by law and by the President's decision and on the issues which are earmarked for its source. Here, the goal is to ensure that the bank is not assigned to the bank at random, that there is no directness with political opinions beyond the requirements of banking, that it is possible to be assigned to the bank in matters which have been divided in a program. The law proposal envisions an autonomous structure such as the Bank of Agriculture and Halkbank, the bank's other public banks, both the structure of the agency and the personnel situation, to be presented accordingly. As a result of the new construction of the bank, it allows personnel to be fully subject to the Business Law, to continue in their institutions, to be transferred to other institutions, even if they want to. However, we consider that experts working at the bank need an additional adjustment to transfer to public institutions and institutions. Expert assistants who have been taken to the Bank of Development for a long time are experts and continue to serve if they pass the acceptance and competency examination of their thesis after a period of growing up under their own legislation. They are not among the experts mentioned in the 36th article of the State Officers' Law, but they are parallel to their work, to their employment, to their upbringing, to their education, and to their work. Therefore, considering that one of the most important problems in our country is a waste of human resources, we consider that during the transfer of the bank's expert staff to other institutions, career expertise should be matched. This will help prevent potential victims of those involved, and will allow for increased public contribution by trained staff. tbmm-2018-10-10sit05spe0173par0198	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I salute your delegation. We begin the first law meeting of our new year of legislation with a law proposal for the Development Bank Anonymous, which is now our agenda. Our proposal consists of a total of 12 articles. Our proposal has been deeply discussed with members of the Plan and Budget Commission, and the General Assembly has come before you. With this law proposal, we aim to basically restructure Turkey's Development Bank Corporation to become a more effective partner in Turkey's growth and development. In this talk, I'm going to talk to you first about a short history and the current structure of the Bank of Development of Turkey, and then I'm going to talk about what our goal is with this legal change and how we're going to build a bank model. Again, with this bill, I'm going to share a number of information on what the Bank of Development in Turkey will now carry out and the model of international development and investment banking with you valued lawmakers. Turkey's Development Bank Anonym Corporation was founded in 1975 as an institution of the Ministry of Industry and Technology under the name of State Industry and Labour Investment Bank -- a short name DİİYAB -- and by the decree of the law. In 1988, the title of the bank was transformed into an Anonymous Company of Turkey's Development Bank with a ruling on another law and was allowed to provide financing to other sectors as well as the industrial sector. Under the decision of the High Planning Board in 1989, Turkey's Tourism Bank Corporation was transferred to Turkey's Development Bank with all its active and passives and was able to finance the tourism sector. Law number 4456, adopted on 14/10/999, and the establishment of the bank have been enforced. In its current case, Turkey's Development Bank is a development and investment bank of the Ministry of Treasury and Finance of the Republic of Turkey, which is subject to special legal provisions and an anonymous company. 99.08 per cent of its capital depends on the Treasury, and 0.92 per cent of its capital is open to the public, and it depends on the real and legal people. With the 8th amendment of the proposed law, we have proposed that the Constitution of Turkey Bank of Development, No. 4456, be abolished and the Bank be restored to an autonomous structure under Article 2 of the Law. In this way, the bank's central, aim, capital structure, organs, accounts, and the distribution of profits will be in the original contract to build an autonomous structure and to prepare the infrastructure needed for more effective banking. Following the 2008 global crisis, the balances in the global economy have not been complete. The increasing imbalances and acceleration of the trade war created by normalisation in monetary policy have increased uncertainty about the global economy. In this respect, the world and especially Turkey are going through a new economic process. The recently announced medium-term plan adopted the new economic programme concept and revealed strong will at the point of implementation of the new economic model. At this point, I regret to say that by looking at the past practices of Turkey's Development Bank, which we consider one of the most important appliances of development programmes, we see that it does not take an active role. Today, there are approximately 550 development banks in the world. 520 of these operate at national level, while the rest operate at regional and global levels. In Turkey, 13 out of 52 banks are development and investment banks. Of these, the Bank of Provinces, Eximbank, Takasbank and Turkey's Development Bank are public-owned development banks. As of the end of 2017, the active size of the Turkish banking sector was 3,253 billion Turkish liras and the credit size was 2.112 billion Turkish liras. Development and investment banks have 5 per cent in total banking sectors of 162.7 billion Turkish liras with active size; 126.7 billion Turkish liras have 6 per cent in credit size. At the same time, Turkey's Development Bank has 2.7 per thousand shares in the banking sector, 2.5 per cent in the banking sector, and 5.5 per cent in the investment banking sector. It has 3.2 per thousand shares in the banking sector and 5.4 per cent in the development and investment banking sector, with a share of 6.9 billion in credit size. As compared to the active size of the Turkish Development Bank, Eximbank is 9.5 times the size of the Eximbank, ilBANK 2.8 times the size, a private development and investment bank, Turkey's Test Development Bank is 3.5 times the size; Eximbank 5 times the amount of equity compared to the bank, ilBANK is 13 times the size of Turkey's Test Development Bank. Normally, we would expect the Bank of Turkey, which we consider to be one of the leading institutions of Turkey's development, to have stronger banking data against these banks that operate more localized, and to have strong competition with the development banks of other countries. We can clearly see that the bank is not at the levels we want when we compare its current structure with the Turkish Development Bank abroad. The active size ratios of decent national development banks to the Bank of Turkey are 20 per cent in China, 15.4% in Germany; 12.7% in Brazil; 16.7% in Korea; 3.7% in Russia; 1.9 per cent in Africa; 0.28 per cent in the Bank of Development in Poland; 4% in Poland; 0.28 per cent in Turkey. Likewise, in Turkey's Development Bank, national development banks' self-source/gayrisafi domestic product rates are 1.56% in China; in Germany, 0.4 per cent in Brazil; in Korea 0.2 per cent in Korea; 0.3 per cent in South Africa; 0.75 per cent in South Africa; in Poland, 0.04 per cent in Turkey's Development Bank. In its current form, we see that the bank is not active in the development process as well as in its lack of effective activity as well as capital and active size. It is clear that the current law cannot fully respond to the need in accordance with the mission we have predicted for the bank. For this reason, a legal arrangement is necessary to ensure that the bank can serve the leading development and investment banks at the equivalent event. With the restructuring process, we are eager to bring Turkey's Development Bank's powerful potential to its goal of development in the coming years. When we look at world examples, development banks operate on the development perspectives of countries by taking to the centre different goals, such as infrastructure, development of industry, regional development, conservation of the environment, innovation, exports and development of institutional enterprises. With this law proposal, our goal is to build a development bank in developed countries, but unfortunately, in some media, they're going to transfer money to their allies. Look, I'll make it clear from here: Turkey's Development Bank, which will become stronger and more efficient with this law proposal, will have a structure that provides technical support and financing for sectors set up in the framework of medium-term programmes and development plans. Our only goal is for Turkey to carry out a strong development move and the Bank of Development to take a leading role in this mission. We consider such ugly accusations against this proposal a sneaky perception operation of some environments who do not want Turkey to develop. Investments in infrastructure, environment, energy, research and development investments, sector efficiency and competition increase investments, financing practices on small- and medium-sized enterprises, investments in primary sectors mentioned in development programmes, investments in primary sectors mentioned in development programmes, investments in foreign-industrial enterprises, and investments in foreign-industrial activities, by building funds, by developing guarantees and risk-sharing programmes, or by developing other development and investments. The bank currently uses 77 per cent of its active size of about 9 billion in debt financing. 58 per cent of loans are given to renewable energy, 18 per cent to manufacturing industry, 14 per cent to tourism, 2 per cent to investments in education and health sectors, and 8 per cent to SMEs. The loan financing given to sectors without specific funding planning will now be a priority for sectors in line with the development targets set. In the same way, we propose the establishment of the Turkish Development Bank's corporate personality, the Turkey Development Fund, in order to provide alternative financing opportunities for strategic sectors and high technology investments that will contribute positively to the development of the country in the middle and long term under the restructuring process. The work and foundation of the Fund will be created by Turkey's Development Fund's Interior Charter. Funds are the most fundamental tools the development banks use to conduct their activities effectively and to catalyze and catalyze private capital. For example, the European Investment Bank Group has established the Risk Capital Fund and the European Investment Fund under the bank to work for the SMEs to support entrepreneurial and innovation. The bank will move forward with a professional team of Turkey's Development Fund, which includes its own administration, and the management of the fund will be supported by the board of directors, investment committee and the advisory board. The reason Turkey Development Fund is involved in this law proposal is that development banks in many developed countries, which are strategically sought to develop, support the ecosystem, which is targeted by investing long-term capital through funds. Turkey's Development Fund, which will be set up as a top fund, will invest capital funds in private companies in medium and long-term capital. The working and essentials of the funds and sub-funds within the fund will be determined by the internal regulations of the fund, the rules of operations, the rules of reporting, the control of the funds and other issues related to implementation. Each subfund will create its own portfolio, with a different investment strategy and investment committee. The investment portfolio of subsidiaries can be made up of companies or direct companies using mutual investment funds and funds established abroad or in Turkey using various financial tools based on self-source or debt. In addition to providing both bank activities and funding with fund managers, a structure will be created that will play an active role in the project from the first phase to the final phase of the project. The 6th amendment of the law proposal brought some exceptions and exemptions to Turkey's Development Fund, while the 7th article also brought exceptions and exemptions to Turkey's Development Bank. The purpose of offering the bank and the funds various exceptions and exemptions is to reduce the additional cost and expense for operating for economic and social development, not for commercial purposes. As we look at examples around the world, the development banks have been given various exemptions and exceptions and privileges so that they can perform these tasks because they take public duties mainly. For example, the Korean Development Bank is not subject to 20 articles of the Korean Banking Law, which includes a total of 67 provisions. Similarly, the Federal Bank of Germany has the same rights as the Federal Bank of Germany, the Federal German Development Bank, for taxation, building construction and rental. In our country, as in Eximbank, institutions are exempt from taxes. The bank does not distribute profits. The terms of the trade law are not applied to the Federal Bank of German Development, except for a few provisions under EU banking regulations. In light of the criticism we've just received, the practice that actually exists in the world's examples has been presented in the law proposal that we're now offering. Finally, amendments to the personnel regime have been proposed in Article 4, Article 1 and Interim Article 2. As of August 2018 in the bank, 481 people are considered retired, and 59 people are part of the SGK according to Law 5510. 34 people from the SGK are employed under contract. In the current case, 54 per cent of all employees working in the bank are already eligible for retirement, and most of them are subject to the Law of the Retirement Ark of the Republic of Turkey, number 5434. The number of experts who deserve retirement is 118, and 111 of them are subject to the pension. As of August 2018, the bank averages the average age of 49. The relevant articles have been proposed to arrange for bank personnel to be employed according to the labour law provisions and to be authorized to the board of directors on matters concerning the employment of personnel. After a certain date, it has been proposed that employees who are not subject to the provisions of Business Law 4857 be appointed to a public institution by the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Services and to be assigned to the appropriate jobs the bank needs until the appointment is carried out. The current board of directors and members' duties to ensure that the transition process needed for the bank's restructuring could not be altered until new ones are elected by the general board. tbmm-2018-10-10sit07spe0355par0032	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of the National Party of Movements on the second part of the 5- rowed Law Proposal under discussion. On this occasion, I salute you all. The most influential type of bank in the development of countries are the development banks. The aim of development banking is to provide long-term funding for companies, to support projects and initiatives that will stimulate the economy, to pioneer direct businesses or participate in new areas that are risky to invest in, to guide entrepreneurs into investment, to provide technical support, to promote investment projects, market research and foreign partnerships, to direct investments into the right areas. As a country that continues the development process, Turkey must improve development banking and expand its volume. As the National Movement Party, we see the need for Turkey's Development Bank to restructure and effectively use it in SME financing and support projects aimed at resolving intergalactic development differences. The 10th Development Plan (Tenth Development Plan)'s goals and policies included the development of development banking, which provides long-term financing for investments. In the banking system and in its understanding and the needs of development banking, the existing Law 4456 cannot fully respond to the need. The Bank of Development has been restructured by the proposal of law and its title has been changed into the Development and Investment Bank Anonym Corporation, it is envisioned to be able to perform development and investment banking functions in a competitive, dynamic and effective way, and by example in other public banks, its administrative and legal body, exceptions and exemptions, and legislation and personnel status are re-determinated. In addition, the establishment of the Turkish Development Fund, the establishment of its legal personality within the bank, and the setting of the procedures and foundations related to these funds and subsidities are envisioned on domestic regulations, and on issues of exceptions and exclusion, on the basis of alternative financing for high-tech investments that will contribute to private capital investments and in the middle, long-term development. The amendment to the Court's audit has been made, based on warnings from the Commission that the Constitution is in violation of Article 165. The offer envisions the employment of bank personnel according to the labour law provisions, the transfer provisions on the status of current personnel and additional pension bonuses aimed at encouraging pensions for those who have earned the right to pension. It is envisioned that personnel who do not want to work under the Employment Law will report to the bank within 90 days following the first general board date, and that they will be assigned to a public institution of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Services until 30 September 2020. The Development Bank is one of our oldest institutions to employ specialists. Those who are part of the Development Bank's professional staff have a four-year degree in grad school and have taken a special competition exam and have been an expert for a period of time on the post-service expertise thesis and competency exam. Hence, its properties are consistent with the characteristics of those listed in Law No. 657 and described as career professions. However, experts listed in Law 657 are not qualified to benefit from 3600 additional indicators and other rights, such as their precedents. In view of the special situation that has arisen due to the bank's restructuring, it would be appropriate for experts who have been given special competition exams to the bank to benefit from the rights of their exemplary expert staff. In the commission talks, EC PARTY Group Deputy President Mehmet Mush Bey said he would work on the issue until the General Assembly stage. I already state that the experts of the Bank of Development must be relieved of this injury during the interim Article 1st meeting.
